// A relocated assertion checks the actual orientation door as well as its destination.
fn readme_reference(name: &str) -> &'static str {
    let map = include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/README.md").split_once("## Reference map").unwrap().1;
    assert!(map.contains(&format!("`guide {name}`")), "orientation omits guide {name}");
    card(name).unwrap()
}

#[test]
fn feeds_card_states_background_contract_and_keeps_recipe_refusals() {
    let feeds = card("feeds").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(feeds, readme_reference("feeds"));
    let contract = feeds.split_once("## Following contract").unwrap().1.split_once("## Boundaries").unwrap().0
        .split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" ");
    for fact in ["newest first", "Your own events appear immediately", "normally within a second", "copies their newest 50 live events immediately, then the rest of a 1,000-event window", "Older posts remain in `posts` and are not copied.", "Unfollow hides that actor on the next read", "your backfill or cleanup is pending", "Removal hides the event immediately", "without prediction"] {
        assert!(contract.contains(fact), "missing {fact}");
    }
    for bad in ["skips a follower", "skip saturated readers", "A skipped follower", "not skipped\nauthors"] {
        assert!(!feeds.contains(bad), "{bad}");
    }
    for code in ["FEED_PULL_CAP", "FEED_PROMOTION_PULL_CAP", "E_RECIPE_RETIRED"] {
        assert_eq!(snapback4_lang::diag::guide_for(code), "feeds");
        assert!(explain_code(code).unwrap().contains("snapback4 guide feeds"));
        assert!(codes().contains(&code));
    }
    for (code, facts) in [
        ("E_RECIPE_RETIRED", vec!["view following: activity", "the engine chooses delivery bounds"]),
        ("E_TIMELINE_FILTER", vec!["view activity", "posts where .parentId = null and not .removed"]),
        ("E_TIMELINE_READ", vec!["following last 20 by .at after c"]),
        ("E_TIMELINE_ACTIVATE", vec!["query home(c: cursor ?): return activity where .actor = viewer or exists follows[viewer, .actor] last 20 by .at after c", "Original event ids are post ids", "--allow-feed-adoption", "5,000,000 copies"]),
        ("FEED_PULL_CAP", vec!["32 shared authors", "rolled back", "new write ID"]),
        ("FEED_PROMOTION_PULL_CAP", vec!["32 shared-author slots", "rolled back", "public", "pullCounts", "server-run query", "used = 32"]),
    ] {
        let explanation = explain_code(code).unwrap();
        for fact in facts { assert!(explanation.contains(fact), "{code}: missing {fact}"); }
        if ["E_RECIPE_RETIRED", "E_TIMELINE_FILTER", "E_TIMELINE_READ"].contains(&code) {
            for old in ["maintain feed: timeline", "events posts where", "NAME[viewer]"] {
                assert!(!explanation.contains(old), "{code}: why still teaches {old}");
            }
        }
    }
    for retired in ["E_RECIPE_BRIDGE", "E_RECIPE_CAPS"] { assert!(!codes().contains(&retired)); }
}

# Totals: count and sum in queries, mutations, jobs and views

Count rows or add up a column with `count` and `sum` inside a query, mutation or job. A view keeps a total per group current on every write, so reading it costs one row.

```quarry
table orders:
  owner: principal
  status: text <=10
  cents: int
  at: time default now
  immutable owner, at
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only
view spentByOwner: sum orders of .cents by .owner
  online only
view spentPerDay: sum orders of .cents by .owner, day(.at)
  online only

query myTotals(since: time):
  open = count(orders where .owner = viewer and .status = 'open')
  recent = sum(orders where .owner = viewer and .at >= since, .cents)
  return { open, recent, lifetime: spentByOwner[viewer], today: spentPerDay[viewer, day(now)], yesterday: spentPerDay[viewer, day(now) - 1] }
```

There are two spellings, and they do not mix:

- **Inside an operation** (query, mutation, job): `count(t where …)` and
  `sum(t where …, .col)`. The parentheses are required; the column is the
  second argument, written `.col`. `min` and `max` take the same arguments.
- **In a view** (declared at top level): `view name: count t by .key` or
  `view name: sum t of .col by .key`, optionally `where …` before `by`
  (`sum t of .col where … by .key`, in that order). Read
  it by its group key, `spentByOwner[viewer]`; a group with no rows reads `0`.
  Omit `by` for one global scalar, `view orderCount: count orders`, and read it
  as `orderCount`. Its authorized empty value is `0` even though zero has no
  physical aggregate row.
  A view over an `online only` table is read online; its own `online only`
  line is optional.

## In a job

A job's totals are ordinary domain reads inside its sweep; write them where a
screen can read them:

```quarry
table readings:
  owner: principal
  value: int
  at: time default now
  immutable owner, at
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only
table sensors:
  owner: principal
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only
table dailyStats:
  owner: principal
  day: time
  n: int
  total: int
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- deny
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only

job dailyTotals(periodStart: time, periodEnd: time):
  for s in sensors by .id:
    n = count(readings where .owner = s.owner and .at >= periodStart and .at < periodEnd)
    total = sum(readings where .owner = s.owner and .at >= periodStart and .at < periodEnd, .value)
    insert dailyStats { owner: s.owner, day: periodStart, n, total }
schedule nightly every 1d at 00:05: dailyTotals
```

`periodStart` and `periodEnd` are the schedule's period, so each run totals
one day. When the job only needs a number a screen shows, a view is usually
simpler than a job: `view perDay: sum readings of .value by .owner, day(.at)`
read as `perDay[viewer, day(now)]`.

## Rows the caller partly reads

An inline total counts only what its caller may read, so `check` refuses one
whose read rule its pins do not decide (`E_QUARRY_BOUND`, or
`E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT` in a mutation): it would be a different number for
each caller, and a capacity check would miss hidden rows. Three exact forms:
pin the deciding column (`.owner = viewer`: the caller's own total); or keep
the total in a view whose read rule names who sees it (the view discloses the
total, never the rows); or, in a mutation, `require` below a view's count.

```quarry
table staff:
  principal: principal
  role: text <=20
  unique principal
  read <- .principal = viewer
  online only
table requests:
  owner: principal
  dept: text <=20
  amount: money USD
  read <- .owner = viewer or exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  online only
view requestsTotal: sum requests of .amount by .dept
  read <- exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
view requestsByOwner: sum requests of .amount by .owner, .dept
  read <- .owner = viewer

query deptTotal(dept: text <=20):
  return requestsTotal[dept] ! else NOT_ALLOWED
query myDeptTotal(dept: text <=20):
  return requestsByOwner[viewer, dept]
```

Finance reads every department's total; each requester reads their own.
`E_QUARRY_BOUND`'s rewrite prints this view for your table, column and group.
A keyed private view stays nullable until its own read rule is proved. For
example, after `require exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
else NOT_FINANCE`, `requestsTotal[dept] + 1` is well typed and a readable
missing group contributes zero. A hidden stored group and a hidden miss both
remain null. A following write or viewer change invalidates the proof before
another keyed read.
A view may omit `by` for a whole-table scalar: `view requestsCount: count
requests`, read as bare `requestsCount`. The scalar still obeys its view read
rule: an authorized absence is zero, while a hidden stored or absent value is

  insert <- .author = viewer and .section.owner = viewer and .section.proposal.state = 'active' and exists sections[.section, proposal = .proposal]
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only

table comments:
  draft: drafts
  section: sections
  proposal: proposals
  author: principal
  body: text 1..2000
  at: time default now
  immutable draft, section, proposal, author, body, at
  read <- (exists sections[.section, owner = viewer] or exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'manager'] or exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'partner']) and (exists proposals[.proposal, state = 'active'] or exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'partner'])
  insert <- .author = viewer and (exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'manager'] or exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'partner']) and .draft.section.proposal.state = 'active' and exists drafts[.draft, section = .section, proposal = .proposal]
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only

fn manager():
  require exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'manager'] else NOT_MANAGER 'Only the proposal manager can do this.'
fn editable(section: sections):
  s = sections[section] ! else NOT_FOUND
  p = proposals[s.proposal] ! else NOT_FOUND
  require p.state = 'active' else PACKAGE_LOCKED 'This package has been signed and locked.'
  return s

query me():
  return staff[principal = viewer]
query team():
  return staff first 50 by .name
query board(c: cursor ?):
  require exists staff[viewer] else NOT_STAFF
  return proposals where .state = 'active' first 50 by .deadline after c
query library(q: text <=100, c: cursor ?):
  require exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'partner'] else NOT_PARTNER 'The proposal library is reserved for partners.'
  return proposals where .state = 'signed' matching q last 50 by .signedAt after c
query sectionsOf(proposal: proposals):
  p = proposals[proposal] ! else NOT_FOUND
  if exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'owner']:
    return sections where .proposal = proposal and .owner = viewer first 40 by .due
  return sections where .proposal = proposal first 40 by .due
query checklist(section: sections):
  s = sections[section] ! else NOT_FOUND
  return checks where .proposal = s.proposal and .section = section first 40 by .id
query draftsOf(section: sections, c: cursor ?):
  s = sections[section] ! else NOT_FOUND
  return drafts where .proposal = s.proposal and .section = section last 50 by .at after c
query commentsOf(draft: drafts, c: cursor ?):
  d = drafts[draft] ! else NOT_FOUND
  return comments where .proposal = d.proposal and .section = d.section and .draft = draft first 50 by .at after c

mutation createProposal(title: text 3..120, client: text 2..80, deadline: time):
  manager()
  require deadline > now else BAD_DEADLINE 'Submission deadline must be in the future.'
  return insert proposals {title, client, deadline}
mutation addSection(proposal: proposals, title: text 2..100, owner: principal, due: time):
  manager()
  p = proposals[proposal] ! else NOT_FOUND
  require p.state = 'active' else PACKAGE_LOCKED
  require due <= p.deadline and due > now else BAD_DUE 'Section due date must be in the future and before submission.'
  assignee = staff[principal = owner] ! else NOT_STAFF
  require assignee.role != 'partner' else NOT_STAFF 'Assign a manager or section owner.'
  return insert sections {proposal, title, owner, due}
mutation addCheck(section: sections, label: text 2..160):
  manager()
  s = editable(section)
  require s.status != 'approved' else SECTION_APPROVED
  return insert checks {section, proposal: s.proposal, label}
mutation setCheck(check: checks, done: bool):
  row = checks[check] ! else NOT_FOUND
  s = editable(row.section)
  require s.owner = viewer or exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'manager'] else NOT_OWNER
  require s.status != 'approved' else SECTION_APPROVED 'Approved sections cannot be changed.'
  update checks[check] {done}
mutation uploadDraft(section: sections, document: file <=5000000 ('application/pdf', 'text/plain', 'text/markdown')):
  s = editable(section)
  require s.owner = viewer else NOT_OWNER 'Only the section owner can upload a draft.'
  require s.status != 'approved' else SECTION_APPROVED
  d = insert drafts {section, proposal: s.proposal, author: viewer, document}
  update sections[section] {status: 'review'}
  return d
mutation comment(draft: drafts, body: text 1..2000):
  d = drafts[draft] ! else NOT_FOUND
  s = editable(d.section)
  require exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'manager'] or exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'partner'] else NOT_REVIEWER
  return insert comments {draft, section: d.section, proposal: d.proposal, author: viewer, body}
mutation approveSection(section: sections):
  manager()
  s = editable(section)
  require s.status = 'review' else NOT_IN_REVIEW
  require exists drafts where .section = section and .proposal = s.proposal else DRAFT_REQUIRED
  require count(checks where .proposal = s.proposal and .section = section) > 0 else CHECKLIST_REQUIRED
  require count(checks where .proposal = s.proposal and .section = section and .done = false) = 0 else COMPLIANCE_INCOMPLETE
  update sections[section] {status: 'approved'}
mutation signOff(proposal: proposals):
  require exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'partner'] else NOT_PARTNER
  p = proposals[proposal] ! else NOT_FOUND
  require p.state = 'active' else PACKAGE_LOCKED
  require count(sections where .proposal = proposal) > 0 else SECTIONS_REQUIRED
  require count(sections where .proposal = proposal and .status != 'approved') = 0 else SECTIONS_INCOMPLETE 'Every section must be approved before sign-off.'
  update proposals[proposal] {state: 'signed', signedBy: viewer, signedAt: now}

  update <- exists staff[viewer] and exists services[.service, published = false]

query searchHymns(q: text <=100, meter: text <=32, musicalKey: text <=12, c: cursor ?):
  return hymns matching q where (meter = '' or .meter = meter) and (musicalKey = '' or .musicalKey = musicalKey) first 30 by .title after c
query serviceList(c: cursor ?):
  if exists staff[viewer]:
    return services last 30 by .date after c
  return services where .published = true last 30 by .date after c
query currentWeek():
  start = day(now) * 1d - ((day(now) + 3) % 7) * 1d
  return services where .published = true and .date >= start and .date < start + 7d first 10 by .date
query serviceEntries(service: services):
  s = services[service] ! else SERVICE_PRIVATE
  return entries where .service = service first 40 by .position { id, position, hymn: hymns[.hymn] }
query hymnUsage(hymn: hymns):
  require exists staff[viewer, role = 'director'] else DIRECTOR_ONLY
  return { total: count(entries where .hymn = hymn and .service.published = true and .serviceDate <= now), recent: count(entries where .hymn = hymn and .service.published = true and .serviceDate <= now and .serviceDate >= now - 30d) }

mutation updateHymn(hymn: hymns, title: text 1..120, tune: text 1..80, meter: text 1..32, musicalKey: text 1..12, scripture: text <=160, topics: text <=200):
  require exists staff[viewer, role = 'director'] else DIRECTOR_ONLY
  update hymns[hymn] { title, tune, meter, musicalKey, scripture, topics }

  require exists staff[viewer] else STAFF_ONLY
  return insert services { title, date, theme }
mutation publishService(service: services, published: bool):
  require exists staff[viewer, role = 'director'] else DIRECTOR_ONLY
  update services[service] { published }
mutation addEntry(service: services, hymn: hymns, position: int 1..40):
  require exists staff[viewer] else STAFF_ONLY
  require exists hymns[hymn] else HYMN_NOT_FOUND
  s = services[service] ! else SERVICE_PRIVATE
  return insert entries { service, hymn, position, serviceDate: s.date }
mutation moveEntry(entry: entries, position: int 1..40):
  require exists staff[viewer] else STAFF_ONLY
  e = entries[entry] ! else NOT_FOUND
  other = entries[service = e.service, position = position]
  if other != null and other.id != e.id:
    update entries[e.id] { position: 0 }
    update entries[other.id] { position: e.position }
  update entries[e.id] { position }
mutation removeEntry(entry: entries):
  require exists staff[viewer] else STAFF_ONLY
  delete entries[entry]

//! A one-hop equality in an online-only table's read rule is the probe of the
//! row that decides it: `.goal.owner = viewer` reads exactly as
//! `exists goals[.goal, owner = viewer]`. Both are false when the reference
//! is null or names no row, and equal otherwise, so the lowering admits the
//! same readers under `and`, `or` and `not`.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#14.3 — wave 4 #8: a hop inside `or` compiles as the probe the diagnostic printed.
use super::*;
use crate::Site;

/// Lower the read rules that hop but do not spell a membership path (those
/// stay `read through`). Synced tables keep `E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP`: sync needs
/// the reader on the row, and its rewrite copies it there.
pub(super) fn lower(tables: &mut BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>) {
    let mut lowered = Vec::new();
    for (name, info) in tables.iter() {
        if info.decl.sync.is_some() || info.decl.ephemeral.is_some() || info.maintained.is_some() { continue; }
        let Some(reference) = super::reference(info) else { continue };
        if paths::declares_membership(info, &reference, tables) { continue; }
        let Some(position) = info.decl.rules.iter().position(|rule| rule.effect == Effect::Read && rule.bindings.is_empty()) else { continue };
        let mut value = info.decl.rules[position].value.clone();
        if rewrite(&mut value, info, tables) { lowered.push((name.clone(), position, value)); }
    }
    for (name, position, value) in lowered {
        if let Some(info) = tables.get_mut(&name) { info.decl.rules[position].value = value; }
    }
}

fn rewrite(expr: &mut Expr, info: &TableInfo, tables: &BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>) -> bool {
    match expr {
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" || op == "or" => {
            let a = rewrite(left, info, tables);
            let b = rewrite(right, info, tables);
            a || b
        }
        Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } if op == "not" => rewrite(value, info, tables),
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, site } if op == "=" => {
            let (hop, other) = if hop(left).is_some() { (left.as_ref(), right.as_ref()) } else { (right.as_ref(), left.as_ref()) };
            let Some((reference, field)) = hop_parts(hop) else { return false };
            match probe(info, tables, reference, field, other, site) { Some(probe) => { *expr = probe; true } None => false }
        }
        Expr::Member { .. } => {
            let site = expr.site().clone();
            let Some((reference, field)) = hop_parts(expr) else { return false };
            let truth = Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Bool(true), site: site.clone() };
            match probe(info, tables, reference, field, &truth, &site) { Some(probe) => { *expr = probe; true } None => false }
        }
        _ => false,
    }
}

fn hop(expr: &Expr) -> Option<()> { hop_parts(expr).map(|_| ()) }

/// `.reference.field`, exactly one hop from the row.
fn hop_parts(expr: &Expr) -> Option<(&str, &str)> {
    let Expr::Member { base, name: field, .. } = expr else { return None };
    let Expr::Member { base, name: reference, .. } = base.as_ref() else { return None };
    matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Focal { .. }).then_some((reference.as_str(), field.as_str()))
}

fn probe(info: &TableInfo, tables: &BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>, reference: &str, field: &str, other: &Expr, site: &Site) -> Option<Expr> {
    let Some(ScalarType { kind: TypeKind::Ref { table: parent }, .. }) = info.columns.get(reference) else { return None };
    let target = tables.get(parent)?;
    if target.decl.ephemeral.is_some() || target.maintained.is_some() || target.through.is_some() { return None; }
    let column = target.decl.columns.iter().find(|column| column.name == field)?;
    // A private field stays refused: the hop's own diagnostic says why.
    if column.written_by.is_some() && !column.readable_by_audience { return None; }
    let kind = &target.columns.get(field)?.kind;
    // The other side: the viewer beside a principal, or a present literal of the field's type.
    let fits = match other {
        Expr::Name { name, .. } => name == "viewer" && matches!(kind, TypeKind::Principal),
        Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Str(_), .. } => matches!(kind, TypeKind::Text { .. } | TypeKind::Enum { .. }),
        Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Number(_), .. } => matches!(kind, TypeKind::Int | TypeKind::IntRange { .. }),
        Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Bool(_), .. } => matches!(kind, TypeKind::Bool),
        _ => false,
    };
    if !fits { return None; }
    let focal = Expr::Member { base: Box::new(Expr::Focal { site: site.clone() }), name: reference.into(), site: site.clone() };
    Some(Expr::Exists {
        read: ReadExpr {
            cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), table: parent.clone(),
            keys: vec![KeyPart { column: None, value: focal, site: site.clone() }, KeyPart { column: Some(field.into()), value: other.clone(), site: site.clone() }],
            seeks: Vec::new(), filter: None, index: None, range: None, matching: None, site: site.clone(),
        },
        site: site.clone(),
    })
}

use snapback4_difftest::{campaign, oracle::Verdict, shrink};
use std::path::PathBuf;
fn main() {
    match command() {
        Ok(failed) => {
            if failed {
                std::process::exit(1)
            }
        }
        Err(e) => {
            eprintln!("{e}");
            std::process::exit(2)
        }
    }
}
fn command() -> Result<bool, String> {
    let args = std::env::args().skip(1).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    match args.first().map(String::as_str) {
        Some("run" | "run-stores" | "run-devices" | "run-lean") => {
            let mut seed = 0;
            let mut workers = 1;
            let mut cases = 1;
            let mut out = None;
            let mut seconds = None;
            let mut i = 1;
            while i < args.len() {
                let value = args.get(i + 1).ok_or("missing option value")?;
                match args[i].as_str() {
                    "--seed" => seed = value.parse().map_err(|_| "invalid seed")?,
                    "--workers" => workers = value.parse().map_err(|_| "invalid workers")?,
                    "--cases" => cases = value.parse().map_err(|_| "invalid cases")?,
                    "--seconds" => { let n: f64 = value.parse().map_err(|_| "invalid seconds")?; if !n.is_finite() || n <= 0.0 { return Err("--seconds must be finite and positive".into()); } seconds = Some(n); },
                    "--out" => out = Some(PathBuf::from(value)),
                    _ => return Err(format!("unknown option {}", args[i])),
                };
                i += 2;
            }
            if cases == 0 {return Err("--cases must be positive".into());}
            let report = campaign::parallel_command(seed, cases, workers, &out.ok_or("--out is required")?, &std::env::current_exe().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?, &args[0], seconds)?;
            println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(&report).unwrap());
            Ok(report.failed())
        }
        // A generated or hand-written source's binder diagnostics (none: "ok").
        Some("compile") if args.len() == 2 => {
            let source = std::fs::read_to_string(&args[1]).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            match snapback4_difftest::execute::compile(&source) {
                Ok(_) => {
                    println!("ok");
                    Ok(false)
                }
                Err(d) => {
                    println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(&d).unwrap());
                    Ok(true)
                }
            }
        }
        Some("replay") if args.len() == 2 => match shrink::replay(&PathBuf::from(&args[1]))? {
            Verdict::Pass => {
                println!("pass");
                Ok(false)
            }
            Verdict::Unsupported(reason) => {
                println!("unsupported: {reason}");
                Ok(false)
            }
            Verdict::Named(outcome) => {
                println!("named: {outcome}");
                Ok(false)
            }
            Verdict::Disagree(f) => {
                println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(&f).unwrap());
                Ok(true)
            }
        },
        _ => Err(
            "usage: snapback4-difftest run|run-stores|run-devices|run-lean --seed <u64> --cases <n> --out <dir> [--workers <n>] [--seconds <n> (stores, devices and lean)] | replay <dir> | compile <file.q>"
                .into(),
        ),
    }
}

                        Ok(Value::Int(if self.device { 1 } else { 0 }))
                    }
                }
            }
        };

        if matches!(builtin, Builtin::ProjectFields { .. } | Builtin::OptionalField { .. } | Builtin::Group { .. } | Builtin::First | Builtin::Last | Builtin::At | Builtin::Concat | Builtin::Slice | Builtin::Sort { .. } | Builtin::MentionTokens | Builtin::Divmod { .. }) {
            self.meter.heap(expression_heap(&value))?;
        }
        Ok(value)
    }

    /// Charge the bytes a result will occupy on the wire.
    pub(super) fn charge_output(&mut self, value: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        // Count the canonical tagged representation without allocating a
        // second result-sized string. A shaped/scalar result cannot be

    available: Condvar,
}

impl AssetTransfers {
    pub(crate) fn enter<'a>(&'a self, asset: &str, principal: &str) -> AssetTransfer<'a> {
        let key = (asset.to_owned(), principal.to_owned());
        let mut active = lock(&self.active);
        while active.contains(&key) {
            active = self.available.wait(active).unwrap_or_else(|poisoned| poisoned.into_inner());
        }
        active.insert(key.clone());
        AssetTransfer { transfers: self, key }
    }
}

pub(crate) struct AssetTransfer<'a> {
    transfers: &'a AssetTransfers,
    key: (String, String),
}

impl Drop for AssetTransfer<'_> {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        lock(&self.transfers.active).remove(&self.key);
        self.transfers.available.notify_all();
    }
}

/// A connection lasts as long as its poll and a grace after it: a client
/// re-polls within milliseconds of an answer, a dropped one never does.
use snapback4_core::engine::{PRESENCE_GRACE, PRESENCE_CONFIRMATION, PRESENCE_REFRESH};

/// The registry of open long polls, per principal: how many are open now,
/// and when the last one ended. A device that holds `/changes?wait` open
/// (every tier does) is connected; nothing else is.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct Live {
    polls: Mutex<BTreeMap<String, (u32, Option<i64>)>>,
    clock: Arc<Mutex<crate::dev_clock::Clock>>,
}

impl Live {
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#19.1 — presence remains server knowledge; development grace uses the same clock as the commit deciding effects.
    fn new(clock: Arc<Mutex<crate::dev_clock::Clock>>) -> Self { Self { polls: Mutex::new(BTreeMap::new()), clock } }

    fn enter(&self, principal: &str) {
        let mut map = lock(&self.polls);
        map.entry(principal.to_owned()).or_insert((0, None)).0 += 1;
    }

    fn leave(&self, principal: &str) {
        let mut map = lock(&self.polls);
        let now = lock(&self.clock).presence_now();
        if let Some(entry) = map.get_mut(principal) {
            entry.0 = entry.0.saturating_sub(1);
            entry.1 = Some(now);
        }
        map.retain(|_, (open, last)| *open > 0 || last.is_some_and(|at| now.saturating_sub(at) < PRESENCE_GRACE.as_millis() as i64));
    }
}

impl Presence for Live {
    fn effect_presence(&self, principal: &str) -> (u64, Option<i64>) {
        let now = lock(&self.clock).now();
        self.effect_presence_at(principal, now)
    }

    fn effect_presence_at(&self, principal: &str, now: i64) -> (u64, Option<i64>) {
        let map = lock(&self.polls);
        let clock = lock(&self.clock);
        match map.get(principal) {
            Some((open, _)) if *open > 0 => (u64::from(*open), Some(now.saturating_add(PRESENCE_CONFIRMATION.as_millis() as i64))),
            Some((_, Some(last))) => {
                let remaining = (PRESENCE_GRACE.as_millis() as i64).saturating_sub(clock.presence_now().saturating_sub(*last));
                (0, (remaining > 0).then(|| clock.now().saturating_add(remaining)))
            }
            _ => (0, None),
        }
    }

    fn connections(&self, principal: &str) -> u64 {
        let map = lock(&self.polls);
        let now = lock(&self.clock).presence_now();
        match map.get(principal) {
            Some((open, _)) if *open > 0 => u64::from(*open),
            Some((_, Some(last))) if now.saturating_sub(*last) < PRESENCE_GRACE.as_millis() as i64 => 1,
            _ => 0,
        }
    }
}

pub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Presence: who is connected right now

Presence reports which principals have an active or recently active connection.
Use it for online indicators and absence-guarded notifications.

```quarry
query connections(p: principal):
  return connected[p]                    -- an int; `exists connected[p]` is the bool
```

`connected[p]` is server-wide. For who is in a room, typing or viewing, use an ephemeral table (guide ephemeral).

`connected[p]` is how many connections a principal holds open on the
server now: a device following the stream keeps a long poll open
(`GET /changes?wait=25&heartbeat=1`), and a poll that answered a moment ago still
counts for 35 seconds, the gap before the next. Heartbeats are JSON whitespace
every 2 seconds before the final object. On a running host, 4 seconds of
silence marks the link offline within 5 seconds; native fetch without streams
uses at most 2-second polls. Browser connectivity events give immediate hints;
authenticated `/probe` confirms recovery. Suspended hosts notify on resume.
It is not a table: nothing
syncs, nothing is written. A mutation uses it to defer an absence-guarded
notification while a connected-looking client is unconfirmed (guide effects); a query reads it for an online mark.
An observed query that may read it re-runs in the background 5 seconds after each answer, keeping
the answer on screen, so an online mark turns about 5 seconds after a first poll or a grace ends.
On a device the word is unknown: a query that reads it is `complete: false`, and the server answers it online.
A mutation stays eligible to predict when presence decides only effects: connection state never changes its rows or result on a successful commit, but can change which effect arms run and therefore its charges, as live versus absent presence already could. The server commits the effects and defers unconfirmed absence decisions until their deadline. The paved `if not exists connected[p]: emit …` form charges the same whether the recipient is held, graced or absent.
Shared boolean payloads and separate positive-presence emits can cost more for
held than graced recipients; a held recipient may get both a deferred absence
effect and an immediate positive-presence effect.
For a proven necessary absence guard in those effects-only mutations, a held poll is unconfirmed: the push is recorded and deferred five seconds from the commit, and only a new poll arriving within that window cancels it. Grace alone keeps its deadline, up to 35 seconds. Cancellation records the `satisfied` outcome `presence_reconnected` (guide effects). The guard decision does not change the value of `connected[p]` in a payload. Only a proven necessary absence guard authorizes cancellation; payload-only reads and alternative guards retain their ordinary decisions and remain immediate. Grace deadlines and recorded reconnects survive restart; `clock advance` makes a waiting effect due along with its grace.
Presence-derived values may use comparisons, `exists`, boolean logic and field values. An infallible point read feeding only an effect payload, including through local bindings, runs and is charged on the server only (`guide prediction`). Other reads, fallible arithmetic or builtins, `new` and refusals controlled by presence keep `predicts: never`, as do conditional values without a fixed-size proof. Prediction charges both safe presence arms apart from the omitted effect-payload work, so it may reach a bound even when the server would fit. If an untaken arm needs a combined guard's value refinement, the device queues without a row; put the data guard outside presence.

## Operations

In tests: `snapback4 clock advance 36s` or `POST /dev/clock/advance` with `{ "duration": "36s" }` closes the up-to-35-second deferral after a poll ends; open polls stay connected, and online client queries read the server's updated answer (guide clock). `snapback4 clock advance 6s` expires a held-poll effect deadline.
`snapback4 run` reads nobody as connected."##;
bare module job. Jobs are not on `api.ts`.

One `use server` in any module (`schema.q` is usual) enables the whole
project; repeating it is harmless. Actions, routes and schedules naming an
action require it. Effects (including secrets, retry, backoff, deadline and
cancellation), jobs, schedules naming a job and service seats need no directive.
See `guide actions` and `guide routes` for the gated declarations.

A job is a sequence of sweeps, optionally preceded by statements. Those
statements run at the start of every chunk under its meter; their bindings
are visible in every chunk. A write belongs to a chunk, never the prelude.
A top-level `for x in table where predicate:` walks a stable domain as the
system principal. Every rule passes; validation, constraints, views and
partitions still apply. It runs under the job ceilings (guide bounds). The cursor commits with the rows, so
a chunk is wholly done or not started and a restart resumes at the next
one; the index's columns are `id` or `immutable`, so nothing moves under
the cursor, and rows inserted past the key captured when the sweep began
are not visited, so a sweep over its own table ends. A refused fixed chunk parks
the job with its refusal (`snapback4 doctor` lists runs). No client calls
a job: a schedule fires it at a period boundary of the server's clock,
never twice for the same boundary across restarts, passing `periodStart`
and `periodEnd` when the job declares them (and nothing else). Boundaries
missed while the server was down coalesce into one run for the latest
period; a schedule first seen fires from its next boundary. `snapback4 run <job> '{..}'` runs one here and now.

A schedule may also target an action: `schedule renewal every 1m as service:billing: remind`. The action must declare only `periodStart: time` and `periodEnd: time`, or no arguments. It runs under that service seat and its action rule, never as the system. Job schedules cannot declare `as`; a job body may use a read-only `as` block (guide actions).

                // An unchanged admitted unique key cannot acquire a collision.

                if old.is_some_and(|old| index_values(index, old) == key) { continue; }

    fn collision_lookup(&mut self, table: &str, name: &str, index: &crate::schema::Index, key: &[Value], except: Option<&str>, effect: Effect) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        let probes = super::rules::rule_probes(&self.schema.tables[table].rules.read);
        self.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, probes)], |runner, allowance| {
            let found = if runner.schema.tables[table].not_before.is_some() {
                runner.lookup_write_occupant(table, name, index, key)?
            } else {
                runner.live_lookup(allowance, table, name, index, key, #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] None)?
            };
            if let Some(row) = &found && Some(row.field("id")?.as_str()?) != except {
                runner.observe_sequence_columns(table, row, &index.columns(), effect == Effect::Insert)?;
                runner.visible_collision(table, row, effect, &format!("unique {}", index.columns().join(", ")))?;
            }
            Ok(found)
        })
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — hidden collisions use the ordinary write-rule refusal
    fn visible_collision(
        &mut self,
        table: &str,
        row: &Value,
        effect: Effect,
        clause: &str,
    ) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let mut image = row.clone();
        let definition = self.schema.table(table).expect("validated");
        materialize_optional_fields(&mut image, definition);
        if self.inactive_write_target(table, &image)? || !self.check_rule(table, Effect::Read, None, &image)? {
            return Err(self.rule_refusal(table, effect, clause));
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn final_exists_holds(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: &Value, constraint: &Constraint) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        let Constraint::FinalExistsOnInsert { table: target, index, key } = constraint else { return Ok(true);
        };
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).expect("validated").clone();
        let has_references = key.iter().any(|term| term.reference_source().is_some());
        let key: Vec<Value> = if has_references {
            // Preserve the structural target probe, but authorize each live
            // row supplying its key before it can confer authority.
            let rule = crate::schema::RuleExpr::Exists { table: target.clone(), index: index.clone(), key: key.clone(), equalities: vec![], provenance: crate::ir::KeyProvenance::Shape };
            for term in key { if let crate::schema::RuleTerm::Field(field) = term { self.provisional_sequence_read |= self.device && table.sequences.contains_key(field); } }
            let Some(key) = self.final_reference_key(table_name, &rule, row)? else { return Ok(false);
            };
            key
        } else { key
            .iter()
            .map(|term| match term {
                crate::schema::RuleTerm::Field(field) | crate::schema::RuleTerm::NextField(field) => {
                    // This inserted image owns a provisional ordinal even if no expression read it.
                    self.provisional_sequence_read |= self.device && table.sequences.contains_key(field);
                    row.field(field).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null)
                }
                crate::schema::RuleTerm::Viewer => Value::Id(self.context.viewer.clone()),
                crate::schema::RuleTerm::Literal(value) => value.clone(),
                crate::schema::RuleTerm::OldField(_) | crate::schema::RuleTerm::Reference { .. } | crate::schema::RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } => Value::Null,
            })
            .collect() };
        self.read_tables.insert(target.clone());
        self.observe_sequence_selection(target, index, &key)?;
        let definition = self.schema.table(target).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing {target}")))?;
        let target_index = definition.index(index).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing {target}.{index}")))?;
        let found = match self.lookup_stored(target, index, target_index, &key, true)? {
            Some(row) => self.live_aggregate(target, row)?,
            None => None,
        };

//! Rule evaluation and the static facts about it that the engine hoists: a
//! rule that is proven true by the scanned prefix (`decided_true`) costs no
//! probes; a rule whose deciding fields are pinned by the prefix (`uniform`) is
//! decided once for the whole range. Copied from Snapback 2's uniformity
//! analysis (LLP 2000.000 §5.1); the certificate that priced these is gone,
//! the analysis is not.

use crate::ir::{Bound, Expr, IndexKeyExpr, Input, Merge, Scan};
use crate::schema::{ColumnType, RuleExpr, RuleTerm, Schema, Table};
use crate::value::Value;
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Visibility {
    /// One verdict serves every row of the scanned prefix.
    pub uniform: bool,
    /// That verdict is `true` before any row is read.
    pub decided_true: bool,
}

pub fn scan_visibility(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> Visibility {
    let Some(table) = schema.table(&scan.table) else { return Visibility::default() };
    let Some(index) = table.index(&scan.index) else { return Visibility::default() };
    let pinned = |name: &str| index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix).find_map(|(part, key)| (part.column().is_some_and(|column| column == name)).then(|| key.scalar()).flatten());
    visibility(&scan.table, table, &table.rules.read, &pinned)

}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.5 — a missing upsert may insert only when the complete held group and its key decide that no hidden target can exist.
pub fn upsert_absence(schema: &Schema, name: &str, index: &str, key: &[Expr]) -> Result<Visibility, &'static str> {
    let table = schema.table(name).ok_or("the target is online only")?;
    let sync = table.sync.as_ref().ok_or("the target is online only")?;
    if sync.horizon.is_some() { return Err("the target has a horizon"); }
    let rule = table.rules.read.device_audience_rule();
    // Public subset sync has no whole-table absence proof. Its group coverage
    // is a separate decision; do not infer it merely from the public read rule.
    if rule.is_uniformly_readable() && sync.audience != crate::schema::Audience::Public { return Err("the key's synced group is not established"); }
    let index = table.index(index).ok_or("the key does not pin readability")?;
    let pinned = crate::small_map::collect(index.components.iter().zip(key).filter_map(|(part, expression)| part.column().map(|column| (column, expression))));
    let visibility = prefix_visibility(name, table, &rule, &pinned);
    if !visibility.uniform { return Err("the key does not pin readability"); }
    Ok(visibility)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#18.1 — simplify self-witnesses, then pre-check only the leading conjuncts whose fields the scope pins, in evaluation order.
pub(super) fn prefix_decision(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    let table = schema.table(&scan.table)?;
    let index = table.index(&scan.index)?;
    let pinned: BTreeMap<_,_> = crate::small_map::collect(index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix).filter_map(|(part, key)| part.column().zip(key.scalar())));
    if table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| pinned.contains_key(&t.reference)) { return Some(table.rules.read.clone()); }
    let rule = simplify_prefix(&table.rules.read, &scan.table, table, &pinned);
    if matches!(rule, RuleExpr::Allow) { return None; }
    let mut terms = Vec::new();
    conjuncts(rule, &mut terms);
    let decided: Vec<_> = terms.into_iter().take_while(|term| {
        let mut fields = BTreeSet::new();
        term.fields(&mut fields);
        fields.iter().all(|field| pinned.contains_key(field))
    }).collect();
    (!decided.is_empty()).then_some(RuleExpr::And(decided))
}

// Flatten only conjunctions, preserving left-to-right short-circuit order.
fn conjuncts(rule: RuleExpr, out: &mut Vec<RuleExpr>) {
    match rule { RuleExpr::And(terms) => for term in terms { conjuncts(term, out); }, rule => out.push(rule) }
}

fn simplify_prefix(rule: &RuleExpr, name: &str, table: &Table, pinned: &BTreeMap<&String, &Expr>) -> RuleExpr {
    let pin = |name: &str| pinned.iter().find_map(|(key, value)| (key.as_str() == name).then_some(*value));
    if rule_known(rule, true, name, table, &pin) { return RuleExpr::Allow; }
    if rule_known(rule, false, name, table, &pin) { return RuleExpr::Deny; }
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::And(terms) => {
            let mut out = Vec::new();
            for term in terms { conjuncts(simplify_prefix(term, name, table, pinned), &mut out); }
            out.retain(|term| !matches!(term, RuleExpr::Allow));
            RuleExpr::And(out)
        },
        RuleExpr::Or(_) => {
            // Use the same self-witness absorption as uniformity, but remove
            // the absorbed field read from the expression we actually run.
            let terms = rule.disjuncts();
            let mut out: Vec<_> = terms.iter()
                .filter(|term| !terms.iter().any(|other| self_probe_absorbs(name, table, term, other)))
                .map(|term| simplify_prefix(term, name, table, pinned))
                .filter(|term| !matches!(term, RuleExpr::Deny))
                .collect();
            match out.len() {
                0 => RuleExpr::Deny,
                1 => out.pop().unwrap(),
                _ => RuleExpr::Or(out),
            }
        }
        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => {
            let inner = simplify_prefix(inner, name, table, pinned);
            negate_required_bool(&inner, table).unwrap_or_else(|| RuleExpr::Not(Box::new(inner)))
        }
        RuleExpr::Ne(left, right) => required_bool_equality(left, right, table, false).unwrap_or_else(|| rule.clone()),
        _ => rule.clone(),
    }
}

/// Required booleans have exactly two values, so their negated equality and
/// inequality forms can share one positive equality identity. Optional bools
/// retain their original expression because `null` is distinct from `false`.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#9 — prefix simplification and guard coverage use the same required-Boolean identity.
fn required_bool_equality(left: &RuleTerm, right: &RuleTerm, table: &Table, equal: bool) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    [(left, right), (right, left)].into_iter().find_map(|(field, value)| {
        let (RuleTerm::Field(column), RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Bool(expected))) = (field, value) else { return None };
        matches!(table.columns.get(column), Some(ColumnType::Bool)).then(|| RuleExpr::Eq(
            RuleTerm::Field(column.clone()),
            RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Bool(if equal { *expected } else { !*expected })),
        ))
    })
}

fn negate_required_bool(rule: &RuleExpr, table: &Table) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Allow => Some(RuleExpr::Deny),
        RuleExpr::Deny => Some(RuleExpr::Allow),
    let (day, three) = (86_400_000, 10_800_000);
    assert_eq!(engine.fire_schedules(day + three + 1_000).unwrap(), Vec::<String>::new());
    assert_eq!(engine.fire_schedules(2 * day + three + 1_000).unwrap(), vec!["everyNight".to_owned(), "summariesEveryDay".to_owned()]);
    assert_eq!(engine.action_counts().unwrap().get("queued"), Some(&1));
    assert!(engine.fire_schedules(2 * day + three + 1_000).unwrap().is_empty());
}

/// Every keyword card's blocks bind clean, alone or beside the language card.
#[test]
fn every_card_compiles_clean() {
    let mut errors = Vec::new();
    for keyword in KEYWORDS {
        let text = card(keyword).unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("no card for {keyword}"));
        let blocks = quarry(text);
        for (n, block) in blocks.iter().enumerate() {
            if std::panic::catch_unwind(|| clean(&format!("card {keyword}, block {}", n + 1), &in_context(block))).is_err() { errors.push(format!("{keyword}: {}", n + 1)); }
        }
    }
    assert!(errors.is_empty(), "unclean blocks: {errors:?}");
    // The relocation preserves compiling adoption instructions for already installed apps.
    let adoption = quarry(include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/FOLLOWING-ADOPTION.md"));
    assert!(!adoption.is_empty(), "installed-app adoption must remain executable");
    for (index, block) in adoption.iter().enumerate() {
        clean(&format!("existing feed adoption block {}", index + 1), &format!("{}\n{block}", snapback4_lang::recipe::following("feed", "").unwrap()));
    }
    assert_eq!(card("grammar"), Some(LANGUAGE));
    assert_eq!(card("quarry"), Some(LANGUAGE));
    assert_eq!(card("nonsense"), None);
    assert_eq!(quarry(LANGUAGE).len(), 22, "Start here, paved introduction, familiar forms, the core program, four more preserved language blocks, field-authority, ephemeral declarations and membership paths");
    // The parts are one program: each compiles only as the whole, which the card runs against the engine.
    let parts: Vec<_> = quarry(LANGUAGE).into_iter().filter(|block| guide::language_part(block)).collect();
    assert_eq!((parts.len(), parts.concat()), (12, guide::language()));
}

/// The package README's Quarry binds clean, and no Quarry hides in an
/// unlabelled fence.

## Child readable by the parent's current roster

`guide rule-recipes current-roster` follows a parent's mutable department.
Write the live rule and a real per-folder product cap. The engine owns the
private audience fields and updates every existing child in the parent's
transaction, including when another mutation writes the parent. The child has
no authored department copy and no application transfer loop.

```quarry
use personas alice, bob, carol
table liveDepartments:
  title: text 1..80
  public 'department names are public'
  online only
table liveDepartmentRoster:
  department: liveDepartments
  principal: principal
  unique department, principal
  immutable department, principal
  read <- .principal = viewer
  sync
table liveRosterFolders:
  department: liveDepartments
  read <- exists liveDepartmentRoster[.department, viewer] or viewer = 'service:ops'
  update <- viewer = 'service:ops'
  sync
table liveRosterFiles:
  folder: liveRosterFolders
  body: text 1..200
  immutable folder
  cap 20 by folder
  read through live .folder
  insert <- allow
  delete <- viewer = 'service:ops'
  sync
mutation addLiveRosterFile(folder: liveRosterFolders, body: text 1..200):
  f = liveRosterFolders[folder] ! else NOT_FOUND
  return insert liveRosterFiles { folder: f.id, body }
mutation transferLiveRosterFolder(folder: liveRosterFolders, department: liveDepartments):
  require viewer = 'service:ops' else OPERATOR_ONLY
  require exists liveDepartments[department] else NOT_FOUND
  update liveRosterFolders[folder] { department }
query liveRosterFile(file: liveRosterFiles):
  return liveRosterFiles[file]
query liveFolderFiles(folder: liveRosterFolders, c: cursor ?):
  return liveRosterFiles where .folder = folder first 20 by .id after c
```

This example prints `N_NO_HORIZON`: twenty files per folder does not bound
files per department or how many departments a device holds. Keep full-current
sync and measure `check --residency`, or choose `online only`; a horizon is
refused for live authority. The cap is a product limit, not an acquisition window. Every parent write
prepays the full schema-fixed fanout, even with no children. A write that
cannot fit rolls back the parent and all children; parent writers queue on
devices. Child-only writes keep the ordinary parent/coverage prediction rules.
Sync carries ordinary row moves in one commit; acquisition catches up before
publishing. Offline devices keep their last applied state. A parent verdict
never substitutes for child coverage, and mismatched held copies take the
server fallback. Service grants stay on the parent and add no device audience.

This first live form requires one immutable required reference and its exact
per-reference cap, ordinary nonexpiring endpoints and nonexpiring probe
graphs. It refuses chains, derived/receipt/private-field endpoints, writing
reference actions on endpoints, nonplain or oversized-key child indexes,
horizons and retained history. Sync requires one exact public, principal or
membership audience; a whole OR-heavy noninvertible parent rule is preserved
with an `online only` child. `N_LIVE_PARENT_COST` warns when the fixed price
already exceeds ordinary ceilings. Use `guide rules` for the complete boundary.

For an existing manual recipe, keep the old copies consistent in a bridge
release, then replace the child's copy/read rule with `read through live` and
remove its transfer loop. Adoption validates correspondence before dropping
columns. Rollback to the manual form fills its audience cells from the current
parent, including formerly dormant cells. Inconsistent or ambiguous policies
refuse atomically; ids do not change. Rebuild devices for `live-parent-copy-v1`.
Bulk seed/import/restore uses one bounded job account; split large input into
coherent parent-plus-children batches, never a partial parent transfer.

//! Flow of server presence through the bound mutation, including loop back edges.
use snapback4_core::ir::{self, Expr, LoopDomain, Program, Stmt};
use snapback4_core::schema::{Execution, ResidencySite, SiteClass};
use snapback4_core::engine::prediction::{total_body, total_expr};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

use super::super::program::setup::{Owner, Ownership, runtime_path};

type Origins = BTreeSet<(String, String)>;
type Env = BTreeMap<String, Origins>;
const REWRITE: &str = "decide only the emit with presence; read it on the server in a service mutation for anything else";

// @ref LLP 3000.007#19.1 — only presence flows ending in effects preserve prediction; every other sink stays server-only.
pub(super) fn inspect(program: &Program, prediction_body: &[Stmt], fact: &mut Execution, ownership: &Ownership, sources: &[(Expr, crate::diag::Site, String)]) {
    let mut flow = Flow { seen: super::effect_reads::uses_presence(program), sites: BTreeMap::new(), consumers: BTreeMap::new(), effect_control: Origins::new(), escaped: Env::new(), ownership, sources, program: &program.name, guards: BTreeSet::new() };
    flow.body(prediction_body, &mut Env::new(), &Origins::new(), "", false);
    fact.connected_effects_only = flow.seen && flow.sites.is_empty();
    if fact.connected_effects_only { fact.prediction_guards = flow.guards.into_iter().collect(); }
    for (key, site) in flow.sites {
        if let Some(consumers) = flow.consumers.get(&key) {
            for consumer in consumers {
                let mut located = site.clone(); located.site = consumer.clone();
                fact.sites.push(located);
            }
        } else { fact.sites.push(site); }
    }
}

struct Flow<'a> {
    seen: bool,
    sites: BTreeMap<(String, String, String), ResidencySite>,
    consumers: BTreeMap<(String, String, String), BTreeSet<String>>,
    effect_control: Origins,
    escaped: Env,
    ownership: &'a Ownership,
    sources: &'a [(Expr, crate::diag::Site, String)],
    program: &'a str,
    guards: BTreeSet<String>,
}

impl Flow<'_> {
    fn reject(&mut self, origins: &Origins, usage: &str, site: &str) {
        for (origin, location) in origins {
            self.sites.entry((site.into(), usage.into(), format!("{origin} {location}"))).or_insert_with(|| ResidencySite {
                // @ref LLP 3000.011.000.000#6 — the existing read site locates the consumer; the reason retains its presence origin.
                site: if site.starts_with("programs.") { site.into() } else { location.clone() }, table: "connected".into(), index: "principal".into(), kind: "presence".into(), class: SiteClass::Server,
                reason: format!("{origin} decides {usage} (read at {location})"), rewrite: REWRITE.into(),
            });
        }
    }

    fn sink(&mut self, value: &Expr, env: &mut Env, control: &Origins, usage: &str, site: &str) -> Origins {
        let mut origins = self.expr(value, env, control, site);
        origins.extend(control.iter().cloned());
        if usage.starts_with("a write") || usage.starts_with("a require") || usage == "a check/refusal" { origins.extend(self.effect_control.iter().cloned()); }
        self.reject(&origins, usage, site);
        origins
    }

    // Assignments replace a slot on this path; branches join their slots. A
    // loop joins its entry and back edge until stable, so a later assignment
    // cannot hide a presence use in the next iteration. Binder slots are unique.
    fn body(&mut self, body: &[Stmt], env: &mut Env, control: &Origins, path: &str, block_value: bool) -> Origins {
        let mut returned = Origins::new();
        for (n, stmt) in body.iter().enumerate() {
            let site = if path.is_empty() { n.to_string() } else { format!("{path}.{n}") };
            let owner = self.ownership.get(&site).map(|owner| owner.kind);
            let block_value = block_value || owner.is_some();
            let before_effect = self.effect_control.clone();
            let empty = Origins::new();
            let control = if owner == Some(Owner::Payload) {
                self.effect_control.extend(control.iter().cloned());
                &empty
            } else { control };
            match stmt {
                Stmt::Const { name, value } | Stmt::Let { name, value } | Stmt::Assign { name, value } => {
                    let mut origins = self.expr(value, env, control, &site);
                    // A presence-selected variable-width value could make later
                    // clones cost more on the server even after both arms paid.
                    if block_value && !fixed_scalar(value) { self.reject(control, "a variable-size conditional value", &site); }
                    origins.extend(control.iter().cloned());
                    env.insert(name.clone(), origins);
                }
                Stmt::Push { target, value } => {
                    let mut origins = self.expr(value, env, control, &site);
                    origins.extend(control.iter().cloned());
                    env.entry(target.clone()).or_default().extend(origins);
                }
                Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => {
                    let mut branch = self.expr(condition, env, control, &site);
                    if !branch.is_empty() {
                        if !total_body(then_body) || !total_body(else_body) { self.reject(&branch, "a fallible or variable-cost arm", &site); }
                        let runtime = runtime_path(self.program, &site);
                        if !runtime.is_empty() { self.guards.insert(runtime); }
                        else { self.reject(&branch, "an arm without a prediction charge plan", &site); }
                    }
                    branch.extend(control.iter().cloned());
                    // Owned short-circuit/coalesce setup computes values;
                    // the remaining source statement arms must
                    // contain only emits. Sinks inside either form still reject below.
                    if !block_value && let Some(usage) = non_effect(then_body, &format!("{site}.then"), self.ownership).or_else(|| non_effect(else_body, &format!("{site}.else"), self.ownership)) { self.reject(&branch, usage, &site); }
                    let mut then_env = env.clone();
                    returned.extend(self.body(then_body, &mut then_env, &branch, &format!("{site}.then"), block_value));
                    let mut else_env = env.clone();
                    returned.extend(self.body(else_body, &mut else_env, &branch, &format!("{site}.else"), block_value));
                    *env = join(then_env, &else_env);
                }
                Stmt::For { binding, domain, body } => {
                    let origins = match domain {
                        LoopDomain::Page(scan) => self.scan(scan, env, control),
                        LoopDomain::ExpressionRange { start, end_exclusive } => {
                            let mut origins = self.expr(start, env, control, &site);
                            origins.extend(self.expr(end_exclusive, env, control, &site)); origins
                        }
                        LoopDomain::DerivedArray { value, .. } => self.expr(value, env, control, &site),
                        LoopDomain::ArgList { name, .. } => env.get(name).cloned().unwrap_or_default(),
                        LoopDomain::Recurse(recurse) => self.sink(&recurse.seed, env, control, "a read key", &recurse.site),
                        LoopDomain::LiteralRange { .. } => Origins::new(),
                    };
                    let mut branch = control.clone(); branch.extend(origins);
rg: snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/mutation.rs: No such file or directory (os error 2)

/// `guide <card> --section <words>`: the title line and one section, found by
/// its number in the index (`0` or `opening` for the text before the first
/// heading), its heading (any case, punctuation ignored), a unique prefix, a
/// unique substring, or headings holding every word. When no heading matches
/// but the words occur, the section holding most of their mentions prints, or
/// every mention when they spread; only words no line mentions refuse, naming
/// the nearest heading by spelling.
pub fn section(name: &str, card: &str, words: &str) -> Result<String, String> {
    let found = sections(card);
    let lines: Vec<&str> = card.lines().collect();
    let title = lines.first().filter(|line| line.starts_with("# ")).map(|line| format!("{line}\n\n")).unwrap_or_default();
    let wanted = words_of(words);
    let print = |start: usize, end: usize| format!("{title}{}", lines[start..end].join("\n").trim_end());
    let opening = opening_end(card, &found);
    if matches!(wanted.as_str(), "0" | "opening" | "intro" | "introduction") {
        return Ok(lines[..opening].join("\n").trim_end().to_owned());
    }
    if let Ok(n) = wanted.parse::<usize>() {
        return match found.get(n.wrapping_sub(1)) {
            Some((_, _, start, end)) => Ok(print(*start, *end)),
            // `1000` is an alias of the bounds card, not its thousandth section.
            None => alias_inside(name, card, words).ok_or_else(|| format!("guide {name} has no section {n}\n{}", section_index(name, card))),
        };
    }
    // An alias that names a section of this card lands there (`subpaths` would match two headings).
    if super::alias_section(words).is_some_and(|(target, _)| target == name) {
        if let Some(landing) = alias_inside(name, card, words) { return Ok(landing); }
    }
    let numbered: Vec<(usize, &(usize, String, usize, usize), String)> = found.iter().enumerate().map(|(n, s)| (n + 1, s, words_of(&s.1))).collect();
    let stages: [&dyn Fn(&str) -> bool; 4] = [
        &|heading| heading == wanted,
        &|heading| heading.starts_with(&wanted),
        &|heading| heading.contains(&wanted),
        &|heading| wanted.split(' ').all(|w| heading.split(' ').any(|h| h.starts_with(w))),
    ];
    for stage in stages {
        if wanted.is_empty() { break; }
        let matches: Vec<_> = numbered.iter().filter(|(_, _, heading)| stage(heading)).collect();
        match matches.as_slice() {
            [] => continue,
            [(_, (_, _, start, end), _)] => return Ok(print(*start, *end)),
            many => return Err(format!("{words:?} matches {} sections of guide {name}: {}; pick one by number: snapback4 guide {name} --section {}", many.len(), many.iter().map(|(n, s, _)| format!("{n}. {}", s.1)).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" · "), many[0].0)),
        }
    }
    // No heading matches. A curated alias naming a section of another card
    // lands there, saying so (`guide wire --section acceptance`).
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.1 — acceptance under wire, statements under language, loops under writes.
    let hits = mentions(card, words);
    if let Some((target, _)) = super::alias_section(words).filter(|(target, _)| *target != name) {
        if let Some(landing) = super::card(target).and_then(|text| alias_landing(words, target, text)) {
            let here = match hits.len() {
                0 => String::new(),
                1 => format!(" (one line of guide {name} mentions it: --find {words:?})"),
                n => format!(" ({n} lines of guide {name} mention it: --find {words:?})"),
            };
            return Ok(format!("No heading of guide {name} is named {words:?}; it is a section of guide {target}{here}:\n\n{landing}"));
        }
    }
    // When the words occur, print the section holding most of their mentions
    // (a strict plurality), or every mention when sections tie for the most.
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.1 — a topic word (audience, React, Reference) prints what it names instead of failing.
    if !hits.is_empty() {
        let mut counts: Vec<(usize, usize)> = Vec::new();
        for &i in &hits {
            let (n, _) = section_of(&found, i);
            match counts.iter_mut().find(|(section, _)| *section == n) { Some(entry) => entry.1 += 1, None => counts.push((n, 1)) }
        }
        let &(best, count) = counts.iter().max_by_key(|(n, count)| (*count, std::cmp::Reverse(*n))).expect("a hit has a section");
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.1 — a plurality lands (`client --section react` listed 4 sections); only a tie lists mentions.
        if counts.iter().filter(|(_, other)| *other == count).count() == 1 {
            let (heading, start, end) = if best == 0 { ("Opening", 0, opening) } else { let s = &found[best - 1]; (s.1.as_str(), s.2, s.3) };
            let share = match (hits.len(), count == hits.len()) {
                (1, _) => "the one line that mentions it".to_owned(),
                (total, true) => format!("all {total} lines that mention it"),
                (total, false) => {
                    let mut others: Vec<(usize, usize)> = counts.iter().copied().filter(|(n, _)| *n != best).collect();
                    others.sort_by_key(|&(n, count)| (std::cmp::Reverse(count), n));
                    let others = others.iter().take(3).map(|&(n, count)| format!("{n}. {} ({count})", if n == 0 { "Opening" } else { found[n - 1].1.as_str() })).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                    format!("{count} of the {total} lines that mention it; also {others} (--find {words:?} prints them all)")
                }
            };
            let note = format!("guide {name}: keyword {words:?} opens {best}. {heading} ({share}).");
            let body = if best == 0 { lines[start..end].join("\n").trim_end().to_owned() } else { print(start, end) };
            return Ok(format!("{note}\n\n{body}"));
        }
        let note = format!("guide {name}: keyword {words:?} appears in {} sections; matching lines:\n", counts.len());
        return find(name, card, words).map(|lines| format!("{note}{lines}"));
    }
    // A whole-card alias no line mentions prints the card's opening.
    if let Some(opening) = alias_inside(name, card, words) { return Ok(opening); }
    // A miss: the nearest heading by spelling (a typo), and the index.
    let mut out = format!("no section of guide {name} is named {words:?}, and no line mentions it");
    let mut near: Vec<(usize, usize, &str)> = numbered.iter().map(|(n, s, heading)| {
        let best = heading.split(' ').map(|h| distance(&wanted, h)).chain([distance(&wanted, heading)]).min().unwrap_or(usize::MAX);
        (best, *n, s.1.as_str())
    }).collect();
    near.sort();
    if let Some(&(_, n, heading)) = near.first().filter(|(best, _, _)| *best <= wanted.chars().count().div_ceil(2)) {
        out.push_str(&format!("; nearest heading: {n}. {heading}"));
    }
    Err(format!("{out}\n{}", section_index(name, card)))
}

/// The most lines `--find` prints before it summarizes the rest.
const FIND_LINES: usize = 80;

import tempfile, subprocess, json
binary='/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w15-rules5/snapback4/target/debug/snapback4'
farm=Path('/home/ccheever/sb4-loop/wave15/apps')
with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix='rules5-admission-') as d:
    h='\\n'.join(p.read_text() for p in (farm/'085-hymn-index-search/snapback').glob('*.q'))
    p='\\n'.join(p.read_text() for p in (farm/'077-rfp-response-desk/snapback').glob('*.q'))
    hl=h.replace('  read <- exists services[.service, published = true] or exists staff[viewer]','  read through live .service')
    old=\"  read <- (.owner = viewer or exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'manager'] or exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'partner']) and (.proposal.state = 'active' or exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'partner'])\"
    cases={'085-original':h,'085-live-cascade':hl,'085-live-restrict':hl.replace('service: services on delete cascade','service: services'),'077-original':p,'077-proposal-only-live':p.replace(old,'  read through live .proposal')}
    for name,source in cases.items():
        cwd=Path(d)/name
        (cwd/'snapback').mkdir(parents=True)
        (cwd/'snapback/schema.q').write_text(source)
        r=subprocess.run([binary,'check','--json'],cwd=cwd,text=True,capture_output=True,timeout=12)
        try:
            result=json.loads(r.stdout)
            print(name, 'status',r.returncode,'ok',result.get('ok'),'problems',[(v.get('code'),v.get('message')) for v in result.get('problems',[])],flush=True)
        except Exception:
            print(name,r.returncode,r.stdout[:200],r.stderr[:500],flush=True)

   880	
   881	
   882	## 13. Wave 15 authorization recipes (RULES5)
   883	
   884	This amendment specifies applications of existing authorization semantics on
   885	LIVE, rather than extending the language or relaxing a rule. It changes no
   886	planner, protocol, adoption, ids, expiry, client contract or engine ceilings.
   887	The commission is wave-15 fix list #4 excluding its two LANG4 note bullets.
   888	
   889	### 13.1 Refusal ladder and probes
   890	
   891	The rules card executes three standalone schemas: an asserted hidden lookup
   892	refuses NOT_FOUND before a later state requirement; a hidden secondary unique
   893	conflict suppresses even an authored `else` code and returns E_RULE; and a
   894	rule `exists` can admit a write using a row that a caller-filtered program
   895	`exists` cannot read. Statement order and rollback still govern, not a new
   896	universal phase order. Successful blind writes can intentionally disclose that
   897	their rule held. No hidden conflict key, clause or row enters the public refusal.
   898	The exact program-probe teaching block intentionally emits one
   899	N_UNREADABLE_PROBE; compile and packed tests pin its code and site. It teaches
   900	caller-visible existence, never global absence enforcement.
   901	
   902	### 13.2 Recipe invariants
   903	
   904	- Readable totals pin the caller's owner scope. They return the complete domain
   905	  total or unavailable; they never expose a subtotal as a complete total.
   906	- Durable handoffs use server `connected` in the mutation and independently
   907	  enforce enrolled-recipient/sender rules. Poll completion retains the existing
   908	  35-second grace. Acceptance during that window is intentional and does not
   909	  assert receipt or future connectivity. Offline calls queue without prediction.
   910	  Ephemeral tables cannot grant durable authority (LLP 3000.017).
   911	- Scheduled publication stores private current draft revision plus approval;
   912	  edits and withdrawal retract the public copy atomically. Editing clears
   913	  approval. Reapproval retracts any previous copy before choosing a new due
   914	  time. The system's stable-id sweep publishes only approved due revisions;
   915	  system jobs bypass source rules, so approval is checked in that job. Ordinary
   916	  callers have no public-copy insert/update rule. Coalescing publishes at the
   917	  next due run, with no exact-instant or downloaded-byte recall claim.
   918	- Private bookings expose a reviewed maintained count, including empty-group
   919	  zero, rather than a caller-filtered capacity count. Admission and insert are
   920	  one writer transaction. All future booking writers must share the check;
   921	  immutable capacity prevents silent shrinking below occupancy. Public count
   922	  deltas are an intentional disclosure, not protection from timing inference.
   923	- The leaderboard keeps attempts private and publishes exact totals only for
   924	  opted-in owners. Every attempt writer refreshes an existing entry atomically;
   925	  opting out deletes it. Names, principals, totals, deltas and relative rank
   926	  become public by consent. Projection alone does not hide source columns.
   927	- Versioned transitions first read the private task, then check expected version
   928	  and the permitted status edge, update and append private history atomically.
   929	  A named old/next rule independently enforces the status edge. Version/history
   930	  invariants belong to every future writer; a failed history insert rolls back
   931	  the update. There is no new generic compare-and-swap or automatic history.
   932	
   933	### 13.3 Three levels and LIVE admission
   934	
   935	LLP 3000.014.000 remains the authority: complete current-parent rule, immutable
   936	required direct reference, exact product cap and bounded atomic maintenance.
   937	The three-level recipe flattens the authority root onto each level, so the
   938	folder and file are separate direct live children of the ordinary project.
   939	The file's immutable project reference is checked against its folder at insert.
   940	It is a hierarchy routing copy, not mutable copied authority. Project transfer
   941	updates both live-child families in one transaction. This is valid only when
   942	all levels share exactly that root's readers.
   943	
   944	A chain of live children remains refused. Wave-15 lane 077 has additional
   945	section-owner grants, so a proposal-only inherited rule would widen access.
   946	Its stable copied ancestor ids plus exact online probes remain appropriate
   947	for those distinct policies. Captured read-through cannot track mutable state.
   948	However, 077's checks can directly inherit their ordinary section's whole
   949	custom rule, whose probe reads the proposal's current state, without making
   950	that section a live child. Existing routing keys used by writers/queries remain
   951	validated. This online policy preserves the section-owner branch.
   952	Lane 085's entries can inherit the complete service rule online once its
   953	writing cascade action is removed and capped explicit child deletion replaces
   954	it. Its unpinned cross-service hymnUsage total remains unproved after the live
   955	conversion; preserve its explicit probe rule or use a separately authorized
   956	maintained view. With cascade retained the live composition stays refused; no reader branch
   957	or reference action may silently disappear. Mutable audience copies remain the
   958	atomic application-maintained choice for unsupported live compositions or a
   959	different intended child audience; immutable copies preserve creation access.
   960	
   961	### 13.4 Qualification and review
   962	
   963	Every added Quarry block is extracted from the installed packed CLI and runs
   964	through the packed client/native packages in the existing publication-rules
   965	qualification path. Tests cover positive, hidden and refused cases, totals,
   966	capacity, consent withdrawal, grace/reconnect, scheduled withdrawal/edit/
   967	reapproval, ancestor transfer and stale/private history. Language guide checks
   968	compile all blocks and allow only the one explicitly taught probe note.
   969	The 077/085 original schemas and live candidates are surveyed read-only and
   970	the precise supported/refused shapes are retained under reports/wave15-rules5.
   971	Paired independent Muse and fresh Codex reviews and disposition are recorded
   972	below when received. LANG4 owns query-audience and N_REQUIRE_REFUSED notes.

LIVE's first form refuses chained `read through live .folder` when the folder
itself inherits a rule. Flatten the authority root as above when all levels
have identical readers. The 077 proposal workflow has additional per-section
owner grants; inheriting only its proposal would widen access. Its checks can
instead use `read through live .section`: the ordinary section's whole custom
rule includes a keyed probe of the current proposal state. This remains online;
the section itself must keep that custom rule rather than become a live child.
Keep copied routing keys still used by queries/writers and validate them on
insert. Bare captured `read through` cannot follow a mutable state flag. A mutable audience copy is
still appropriate for unsupported live compositions, such as timed parents,
or when the child intentionally has a different audience; maintain it
atomically through every parent writer (`current-roster`, Manual copy
alternative). An immutable audience copy instead preserves creation-time access.

The 085 hymn entries share their service's complete rule and can use
`read through live .service` online after removing the writing cascade action
from that endpoint; delete the capped entries explicitly before their service.
Its public-or-staff rule stays online. The full lane's cross-service hymnUsage
total still needs its existing explicit probe form or a separately authorized
maintained view: the planner cannot prove that total uniform after direct LIVE
conversion. Keeping cascade semantics unchanged also requires the existing
explicit keyed probes. LIVE never silently
drops a reader branch or a reference action.

For the distinct per-section policy, keep that policy on the ordinary middle
table. This reduced 077 form retains the owner's active-only access and the
partner's signed-package access, with no authored ancestor copy on the check:
    await p.failed('closeLadderTicket','alice',{ticket:'absent'},'NOT_FOUND');

   296	total still needs its existing explicit probe form or a separately authorized
   297	maintained view: the planner cannot prove that total uniform after direct LIVE
   298	conversion. Keeping cascade semantics unchanged also requires the existing
   299	explicit keyed probes. LIVE never silently
   300	drops a reader branch or a reference action.
