   302	For the distinct per-section policy, keep that policy on the ordinary middle
   303	table. This reduced 077 form retains the owner's active-only access and the
   304	partner's signed-package access, with no authored ancestor copy on the check:
   305	
   306	```quarry
   307	use personas alice, bob, carol
   308	table policyStaff:
   309	  principal: principal
   310	  role: enum('owner', 'partner')
   311	  unique principal
   312	  read <- .principal = viewer
   313	  online only
   314	table policyProposals:
   315	  state: enum('active', 'signed') default 'active'
   316	  read <- exists policyStaff[viewer]
   317	  update <- exists policyStaff[viewer, role = 'partner']
   318	  online only
   319	table policySections:
   320	  proposal: policyProposals
   321	  owner: principal
   322	  immutable proposal, owner
   323	  read <- (.owner = viewer or exists policyStaff[viewer, role = 'partner']) and (exists policyProposals[.proposal, state = 'active'] or exists policyStaff[viewer, role = 'partner'])
   324	  online only
   325	table policyChecks:
   326	  section: policySections
   327	  body: text 1..80
   328	  immutable section
   329	  cap 20 by section
   330	  read through live .section
   331	  insert <- exists policyStaff[viewer, role = 'partner']
   332	  online only
   333	mutation addPolicyCheck(section: policySections, body: text 1..80):
   334	  return insert policyChecks { section, body }
   335	mutation signPolicyProposal(proposal: policyProposals):
   336	  update policyProposals[proposal] { state: 'signed' }
   337	query policyCheck(check: policyChecks):
   338	  return policyChecks[check]
   339	```
   340	
   341	Changing a probed proposal state changes the online read verdict without
   342	rewriting section/check rows. This differs from changing a policy input stored
   343	on the immediate parent: LIVE maintains that input's private child copy in the
   344	same parent transaction. Both keep the whole authored policy.
   345	
   346	## Versioned status transitions with private history
   347	
   348	`guide rule-recipes private-history` uses an expected version to reject a stale
   349	transition, a named old/next rule for the permitted edge, and an append-only
   350	owner-readable history row in the same transaction. No public status/history
   351	aggregate leaks the private transition. A hidden task is `NOT_FOUND` before
   352	the program's `STALE_VERSION` or `BAD_TRANSITION` checks.
   353	
   354	```quarry
   355	use personas alice, bob, carol
   356	table versionedTasks:
   357	  owner: principal
   358	  status: enum('draft', 'submitted', 'approved') default 'draft'
   359	  version: int default 1
   360	  immutable owner
   361	  read <- .owner = viewer
   362	  insert <- .owner = viewer and .status = 'draft' and .version = 1
   363	  update (old, next) <- old.owner = viewer and next.owner = old.owner and ((old.status = 'draft' and next.status = 'submitted') or (old.status = 'submitted' and next.status = 'approved'))
   364	  online only
   365	table privateStatusHistory:
   366	  task: versionedTasks
   367	  owner: principal
   368	  version: int
   369	  status: enum('draft', 'submitted', 'approved')
   370	  at: time default now
   371	  unique task, version
   372	  immutable task, owner, version, status, at
   373	  read <- .owner = viewer
   374	  insert <- .owner = viewer and exists versionedTasks[.task, owner = .owner, version = .version, status = .status]
   375	  online only
   376	mutation newVersionedTask():
   377	  return insert versionedTasks { owner: viewer }
   378	mutation transitionTask(task: versionedTasks, expected: int, status: enum('draft', 'submitted', 'approved')):
   379	  t = versionedTasks[task] ! else NOT_FOUND
   380	  require t.version = expected else STALE_VERSION
   381	  require (t.status = 'draft' and status = 'submitted') or (t.status = 'submitted' and status = 'approved') else BAD_TRANSITION
   382	  update versionedTasks[task] { status, version: t.version + 1 }
   383	  insert privateStatusHistory { task, owner: viewer, version: t.version + 1, status }
   384	query versionedTask(task: versionedTasks):
   385	  return versionedTasks[task]
   386	query statusHistory(task: versionedTasks, c: cursor ?):
   387	  return privateStatusHistory where .task = task and .owner = viewer first 20 by .version after c
   388	```
   389	
   390	The expected-version check and increment are application invariants in this
   391	mutation; the table transition rule independently authorizes the status edge.
   392	Keep every future writer on the same version/history path. History insert
   393	failure rolls the status update back. `LANG4` owns the query-audience and
   394	`N_REQUIRE_REFUSED` diagnostics for these workflows.
    assert.equal(await p.read('myAttemptTotal','bob'),100);assert.equal(await p.read('myAttemptTotal','carol'),0);
  });

    await p.seed([
      {table:'policyStaff',row:{id:'a',principal:'dev:alice',role:'owner'}},
      {table:'policyStaff',row:{id:'b',principal:'dev:bob',role:'partner'}},
      {table:'policyProposals',row:{id:'proposal',state:'active'}},
      {table:'policySections',row:{id:'section',proposal:'proposal',owner:'dev:alice'}},
    ]);

  await t.test('versioned transitions reject stale and hidden targets and retain private atomic history', async t=>{
    const p=await project(t,'table versionedTasks:');
    const row=await p.sent('newVersionedTask','alice');
    await p.failed('transitionTask','bob',{task:row.id,expected:1,status:'submitted'},'NOT_FOUND');
    await p.failed('transitionTask','alice',{task:row.id,expected:1,status:'approved'},'BAD_TRANSITION');
    await p.sent('transitionTask','alice',{task:row.id,expected:1,status:'submitted'});
    await p.failed('transitionTask','alice',{task:row.id,expected:1,status:'approved'},'STALE_VERSION');
    await p.sent('transitionTask','alice',{task:row.id,expected:2,status:'approved'});
    const history=await p.read('statusHistory','alice',{task:row.id,c:null});
    assert.deepEqual(history.map(r=>[r.version,r.status]),[[2,'submitted'],[3,'approved']]);
    assert.deepEqual(await p.read('statusHistory','bob',{task:row.id,c:null}),[]);
    assert.equal((await p.read('versionedTask','alice',{task:row.id})).version,3);
    const collision=await p.sent('newVersionedTask','alice');
    await p.seed([{table:'privateStatusHistory',row:{id:'future',task:collision.id,owner:'dev:alice',version:2,status:'submitted',at:Date.now()}}]);
    await p.failed('transitionTask','alice',{task:collision.id,expected:1,status:'submitted'},'E_UNIQUE');
    assert.equal((await p.read('versionedTask','alice',{task:collision.id})).version,1,'history collision rolls the preceding update back');
  });

//! Refusals: every failure the engine reports is one envelope
//! `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}` (LLP 2000.000 §5.1), and
//! the same envelope is what a device shows and what `why` explains.

use crate::schema::Effect;
use crate::value::ValueError;
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use thiserror::Error;

/// The refusal envelope. `code` and `family` are stable identifiers; `rewrite`
/// is text the author can paste; `guide` is the keyword card to read.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Refusal {
    pub code: String,
    pub family: String,
    pub message: String,
    /// Original constraint text for live development refusals; durable receipts and replays omit this row-derived detail.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub detail: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub site: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub rewrite: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub guide: Option<String>,
    /// The author's own code, for `require` and named unique refusals.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub rule: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::ops::Not::not")]
    pub retryable: bool,
}

impl Refusal {
    pub fn new(code: impl Into<String>, family: impl Into<String>, message: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self { code: code.into(), family: family.into(), message: message.into(), detail: None, site: None, rewrite: None, guide: None, rule: None, retryable: false }
    }
    pub fn site(mut self, site: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        self.site = Some(site.into());
        self
    }
    pub fn rewrite(mut self, rewrite: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        self.rewrite = Some(rewrite.into());
        self
    }
    pub fn guide(mut self, guide: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        self.guide = Some(guide.into());
        self
    }
}

impl std::fmt::Display for Refusal {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "{}: {}", self.code, self.message)?;
        if let Some(site) = &self.site {
            write!(f, " (at {site})")?;
        }
        if let Some(rewrite) = &self.rewrite {
            write!(f, "\n  rewrite: {rewrite}")?;
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

/// Which meter cut a program (LLP 2000.000 §6).
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
pub enum MeterKind {
    Examined,
    IndexEntries,
    RuleProbes,
    Steps,
    HeapBytes,
    OutputBytes,
    RowsWritten,
}

impl MeterKind {
    pub fn word(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Self::Examined => "examined rows",
            Self::IndexEntries => "index entries",
            Self::RuleProbes => "rule probes",
            Self::Steps => "steps",
            Self::HeapBytes => "heap bytes",
            Self::OutputBytes => "output bytes",
82:            Self::RuleProbes => "rule probes",

219:            Self::Rule { .. } | Self::AuthorizationBudget { .. } | Self::Auth(_) => "auth",

  async function project(t, marker, sourceBlocks = recipes, {schedules = false} = {}) {
    const source = sourceBlocks.find(s=>s.includes(marker)); assert.ok(source, marker);
    const cwd = join(dir, marker.replace(/\W/g,'_')); mkdirSync(join(cwd,'snapback'),{recursive:true});
    writeFileSync(join(cwd,'snapback/schema.q'), source);
    const checked = JSON.parse(invoke(process.execPath,[cliPath,'check','--json'],cwd,env));
    assert.equal(checked.ok,true);

      assert.doesNotMatch(JSON.stringify(notes),/last 500/);

    t.after(()=>host.stop());

      const response = await fetch(host.url+path,{method:'POST',headers:{'content-type':'application/json','x-snapback-persona':who},body:JSON.stringify(body),signal:AbortSignal.timeout(15000)});
      return response.json();
  await t.test('hidden unique conflicts redact even the authored code', async t=>{
    const p=await project(t,'table ladderKeys:',rules);
    await p.sent('claimLadderKey','bob',{key:'private'});
    const hidden=await p.failed('claimLadderKey','alice',{key:'private'},'E_RULE');
    assert.doesNotMatch(JSON.stringify(hidden.why),/KEY_TAKEN|private/);
    await p.failed('claimLadderKey','bob',{key:'private'},'KEY_TAKEN');
    await p.sent('claimLadderKey','alice',{key:'free'});
  });
  await t.test('rule and program exists deliberately see different populations', async t=>{
    assert.equal((await p.read('handoffs','alice',{c:null})).length,2);
  });
  await t.test('scheduled publication withdraws, invalidates approval on edit and reapproves', async t=>{
    const p=await project(t,'table scheduledDrafts:',recipes,{schedules:true});
    const d=await p.sent('newScheduledDraft','alice',{body:'v1',publishAt:Date.now()+60000});
    const tick=async()=>{const reply=await p.post('/dev/clock/advance','alice',{duration:'2m'});assert.ok(!reply.why,JSON.stringify(reply));};
    await p.sent('approveScheduledDraft','alice',{draft:d.id,publishAt:Date.now()+60000});
    assert.equal(await p.read('scheduledPublication','bob',{draft:d.id}),null);
    await tick();assert.equal((await p.read('scheduledPublication','bob',{draft:d.id})).body,'v1');
    await p.failed('withdrawScheduledDraft','bob',{draft:d.id},'NOT_FOUND');
    await p.sent('withdrawScheduledDraft','alice',{draft:d.id});await tick();

No blocking findings remain in the latest observed revision.

Three defects found during review were corrected in concurrent edits:

- The hidden-conflict test now permits the static table name in the ordinary ambiguous `E_RULE` envelope while checking that the authored unique code and conflicting value remain hidden.
- The scheduled-publication test now enables schedules.
- The 085 guidance now acknowledges both actual boundaries: converting its read rule leaves `hymnUsage` totals refused with `E_QUARRY_BOUND`; adding an aggregate over the live endpoint is also refused with `E_READ_THROUGH`.

The 077 guidance correctly preserves section-specific authority. Its reduced example inherits the ordinary section’s whole rule, retaining the live proposal-state probe without creating a prohibited LIVE chain. The flattened three-level recipe likewise respects the governing direct-reference and cap requirements.

I found no remaining correctness, privacy, authority, or transactional defect in the recipes. The distinction between rule probes and caller-visible probes is explicit, aggregate disclosures are intentional, and system publication checks approval independently.

- No checkout edits, Cargo invocation, or full `test.sh` run.
